recognized  for  the  three  months  and  fiscal  year  ended  March  31,  2026.  On  an  as-converted  basis  and  subject  to  the  liquidation  preference  of  the  Convertible  Preferred  Shares  and  the  Preference  Shares  the 

non-controlling  interest  represents  a 33.1%  equity  equivalent  [three  months  and  fiscal  year  ended  March  31,  2025  – 33.1%].

significant  items (1) . Under  IFRS,  the  Company  recorded  a net  loss  attributable  to  common  shareholders  for  fiscal  2026.  Accordingly,  diluted  loss  per  share  equals  basic  loss  per  share,  as  all  instruments  that 

could  potentially  dilute  earnings  per  share  (EPS)  were  excluded  from  the  calculation  due  to  their  anti-dilutive  effect.  For  Q4/26,  the  diluted  EPS  and  net  income  attributable  to  common  shareholders  (under 

IFRS  and  on  an  adjusted  basis  excluding  significant  items (1) ),  as  well  as  fiscal  2026  adjusted  EPS  excluding  significant  items (1) , are  calculated  after  deducting  paid  and  accrued  dividends  on  the  Convertible 

Preferred  Shares  and  Preference  Shares  issued  by  CGWM  UK  in  determining  net  income  attributable  to  CGGI  shareholders. 

(7)  Fiscal  2026  Other  Revenue  includes  the  gain  on  the  disposal  of  the  US  wholesale  market  making  business  completed  on  November  7,  2025.

## ABOUT CANACCORD GENUITY 

## GROUP INC. 

Through its principal subsidiaries, Canaccord Genuity 

Group Inc. (the Company) is a leading independent, 

full-service financial services firm, with operations 

in two principal segments of the securities industry: 

wealth management and capital markets. Since its 

establishment in 1950, the Company has been driven 

by an unwavering commitment to building lasting 

client relationships. We achieve this by generating 

value for our individual, institutional and corporate 

clients through comprehensive investment solutions, 

brokerage services and investment banking services. 

The Company has wealth management offices located 

in Canada, the UK, Guernsey, Jersey, the Isle of Man 

and Australia. The Company’s international capital 

markets division operates in North America, the 

UK & Europe, Asia and Australia. 

Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. is publicly traded 

under the symbol CF on the TSX.

# LETTER FROM 

# THE CHAIRMAN & CEO 

Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. Fiscal 2026 Annual Report 

# “Our business is built around 

# an important purpose: 

# helping growth companies 

# and investors access advice, 

# capital, and opportunities 

# to advance their goals.” 

4

Dan Daviau , Chairman & CEO 

Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. Fiscal 2026 Annual Report  5

Through this, we stayed focused on what matters most: 

delivering market-leading advice and execution for 

clients, investing selectively in the platform, managing 

risk carefully, and making disciplined capital allocation 

decisions with a long-term view. 

Firm-wide revenue of $2.2 billion was a new record 

and reflects an operating model designed to protect 

shareholder value across market cycles. We grew 

market share across several businesses and invested 

in products, people and technology, while upholding 

strong risk management and compliance. While a more 

supportive market backdrop contributed to top-line 

growth, operating discipline drove significantly stronger 

profitability. Adjusted net income attributable to common 

shareholders increased 115% year over year, and, despite 

higher costs associated with strategic initiatives and 

certain one-time expenses, we delivered meaningful 

pre -tax margin expansion of 3.5 percentage points. 

We invested in and scaled our global wealth 

management platform in fiscal 2026, building on 

prior acquisitions while driving organic growth. 

Our focus on targeted recruitment, expanded product 

offerings and deeper client partnerships is strengthening 

our ability to attract and retain assets, drive net inflows and 

improve the quality of earnings. This translated into record 

performance, with revenue of $1.1 billion and adjusted 

pre -tax net income of $195 million. Client assets increased 

to a record $148 billion, supported by market gains, stronger 

client engagement and positive net inflows. 

Performance in each region was driven by a distinct set of 

factors. In the UK & Crown Dependencies, organic growth 

was supported by synergies from prior acquisitions and 

expanded financial planning capabilities, enhancing 

the client and advisor experience, increasing recurring 

revenue and positioning the business for margin expansion 

as platform investment costs normalize. In Canada, 

growth reflected increased client activity, positive inflows 

and a more active new issue environment, complemented 

by selective recruiting and advisor teaming to deepen 

coverage and better serve increasingly complex client 

needs. We see continued growth potential as domestic 

consolidation among competitors creates opportunities 

to gain market share, while enhanced platform scale 

provides additional pathways through complementary 

products and alternative wealth channels. 

Our Australian wealth management business delivered 

exceptional full-year results, supported by solid underlying 

growth, strong recruiting momentum and increased new 

issue activity. The acquisition of Wilsons Advisory added 

approximately 60 talented advisors, giving the business 

a truly national footprint in an important region. Since 

welcoming our Wilsons colleagues, we have focused on 

integration, client continuity and capturing the long-term 

opportunities created by our expanded scale. We continue 

to see a compelling opportunity to build our Australian 

wealth franchise into the leading independent wealth 

manager in the region. 

Results in our capital markets division reflected the 

disciplined preparation that enabled our teams to 

act decisively as opportunities emerged. 

Despite volatility driven by trade-related uncertainty, 

interest rate expectations, geopolitical developments 

and AI-related disruption, we delivered for our clients 

while driving stronger revenue and earnings momentum. 

Revenue in this division increased 26% year over year 

to $1.0 billion, with pre-tax net income contribution 

increasing by more than 200%. 

# Fellow shareholders, 

# Fiscal 2026 tested conviction and rewarded focus, reinforcing the importance of preparation and 

# the strength of our partnership culture. The year began against an uncertain market backdrop, 

# shaped by shifting trade policy, evolving interest rate uncertainty and geopolitical developments. 

# Conditions became more constructive as the year progressed, although bouts of volatility 

# reinforced the importance of preparation, disciplined execution and an unwavering client focus. Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. Fiscal 2026 Annual Report 6

Fiscal 2026 investment banking revenue increased 

93% year over year and represented 39% of global 

capital markets revenue, driven by substantial growth 

in corporate financing activity in all our regions, led by 

Canada and Australia. Activity improved across our 

core sectors, with metals and mining benefiting from 

unusually strong conditions during the year. While we 

expect these conditions to moderate, the mining sector 

is becoming less purely cyclical, supported by long-

term demand drivers including electrification, energy 

transition infrastructure, battery metals and supply chain 

security. These trends are constructive for CG, given 

our leading market position, deep sector expertise, and 

cross-border capabilities. 

While our advisory activity outpaced the broader market 

in recent years, momentum was abruptly affected early 

in fiscal 2026, as policy and trade uncertainty delayed 

deal completions across many of the small- and mid-cap 

sectors we serve. Conditions improved over the course 

of the year, although the recovery in these segments was 

gradual. Fiscal 2026 advisory revenue was in line with the 

prior year and contributed 29% of total capital markets 

revenue. Performance was led by our US business, with 

strong deal activity in the technology sector, as well as 

improved contributions across other key areas, including 

metals and mining, where we leveraged shared expertise 

and relationships across Canada, Australia and the UK to 

drive results. 

Consistent with our commitment to providing 

differentiated advice, we also took steps to enhance 

capabilities complementary to our core focus sectors. 

Our acquisition of CRC-IB expands our expertise and 

relationships in renewable energy and energy transition, 

strengthening our ability to advise clients in an increasingly 

important area of global markets. 

At the same time, we continued to simplify and sharpen 

our platform. In November, we completed the sale of 

our US wholesale market making business, aligning our 

footprint more closely with our strategic priorities and 

reinforcing our focus on businesses where we see the 

strongest opportunities for long-term growth. 

Looking ahead, we expect conditions to remain generally 

constructive, supported by improving small- and mid-cap 

equity markets and a continued recovery in capital raising 

and advisory activity across our core sectors. While IPO 

activity has not yet fully recovered, stronger investment 

banking activity within our Canadian and Australian 

wealth businesses points to improving risk appetite. We 

remain mindful, however, that geopolitical risks, uneven 

interest rate expectations and rising energy prices could 

contribute to variability in near-term market conditions, 

with potential implications for inflation and overall 

economic growth. 

At the core of our performance is our 

partnership culture, which supports a long-term 

approach to serving our clients and creating value 

for shareholders. 

In fiscal 2026, our limited partnership grew to hold 

just over 14% of outstanding common shares. As a 

permanent capital structure, the partnership is a core 

differentiator, promoting durable alignment among our 

top employees, clients and shareholders while reinforcing 

an entrepreneurial mindset, sound risk management and 

long-term performance. 

In total, approximately 44% of our outstanding common 

shares are now held by our leadership, board members and 

employees. Over time, we intend to broaden participation 

in the employee partnership, while maintaining thoughtful 

allocation and alignment with our strategic priorities. 

During the year, we resolved previously disclosed 

regulatory enforcement matters related to non-core 

trading operations in our US capital markets business. 

While these matters did not implicate any other US 

businesses or CG operations in other geographies, 

addressing them required significant, detailed and 

demanding work. The related financial settlement and 

remediation costs impacted profitability in our US 

business during the year, but we expect performance to 

normalize and strengthen over time as our business mix 

continues to evolve. 

Beyond resolving these matters, we have materially 

invested over several years to strengthen our compliance 

framework, controls, and oversight capabilities across the 

organization. The framework we have today is stronger, 

more consistent and better positioned for the future, 

reflecting the expertise, persistence and commitment of 

the teams who led this work. We are also investing in technology, including 

artificial intelligence, to strengthen how we 

serve clients and run our business. 

Our focus is practical: using AI tools to improve efficiency, 

enhance the speed and quality of our insights, and 

strengthen controls and operational risk management. 

We are approaching this opportunity responsibly, with 

clear governance, appropriate data protections and 

meaningful human oversight. Where AI supports 

decision-making or client-facing processes, we prioritize 

transparency, privacy, testing and ongoing monitoring, 

with safeguards to protect client information and mitigate 

bias. Most importantly, these tools are designed to 

support our people, not replace the trusted relationships 

that define our business. 

Our business is built around an important purpose: 

helping growth companies and investors access 

advice, capital, and opportunities to advance 

their goals. 

The markets we serve drive innovation, employment 

and economic activity, yet they are often underserved 

by traditional banking channels. I am proud of the work 

our 3,100-plus colleagues do every day to support our 

clients’ success and grateful for their unwavering focus 

and dedication. 

Looking ahead, our priorities are straightforward: 

execute for our clients, deepen partnerships, 

continue to strengthen our risk management 

and compliance framework, and allocate 

capital thoughtfully. 

We also remain committed to evaluating strategic 

options, with the objective of maximizing long-term value 

for shareholders while maintaining continuity and high 

standards of service for clients. It is a privilege to serve 

as your Chairman, and I am grateful for the confidence of 

our shareholders, the partnership of our Global Operating 

Committee, and the guidance and counsel of our Board 

as we advance these priorities. 

Dan Daviau 

Chairman & CEO 

Canaccord Genuity Group Inc.

CANADA 

Our Canadian wealth management business delivered record annual 

revenue of $460 million, an increase of 23% year over year, supported 

by record commissions and fees revenue and a doubling of corporate 

financing revenue from the prior year. Client assets increased 30% to 

an all-time high of $56 billion, largely reflecting higher market values, 

advisor recruitment and positive net flows, representing 11% of 

opening assets under administration. The business remained focused 

on increasing contributions from fee-generating accounts, which 

represented 51% of total client assets in this business, down modestly 

from the prior year, reflecting the higher proportion of commission-

based assets amid strong transaction volumes. Pre-tax net income (1) 

in this business increased 61% to $69 million, supporting margin 

expansion, while normalized EBITDA (1)  improved 41% to $96.8 million, 

representing an EBITDA margin of 21%.

AUSTRALIA 

Our Australian wealth management business delivered exceptional 

growth in fiscal 2026, supported by strong underlying performance, 

increased client activity and the acquisition of Wilsons Advisory, which 

added meaningful scale and established a truly national footprint. 

Revenue increased 87% year over year to a record $150 million, 

primarily reflecting an 80% increase in commissions and fees revenue 

to $128 million. The business also benefited from stronger corporate 

financing activity, with revenue of $21 million, up 164% year over year, 

reflecting improved market conditions and increased participation 

in client transactions. Client assets ended the year at $18 billion, 

up 113%, supported by market growth, advisor recruitment and 

approximately $7.0 billion of client assets contributed by Wilsons. 

Pre-tax net income (1)  increased 210% to a record $15.3 million. 

Recruitment remained strong, with 10 new advisors joining across 

key markets in fiscal 2026.

Corporate financing activity improved substantially 

across our core focus sectors in fiscal 2026, supporting 

one of our strongest annual investment banking 

performances on record.  Investment banking was the 

largest contributor to consolidated capital markets 

revenue in fiscal 2026. Revenue increased 93% year over 

year to $412 million, representing our third-strongest 

annual result on record. Mining-related activity was a key 

driver, accounting for 51% of total investment banking 

revenue, supported by record gold prices and solid 

demand for industrial metals. While IPO activity has not 

yet fully recovered, demand for high-quality new issues 

is improving, supported by a solid pipeline of businesses 

seeking access to the public markets. The supportive 

backdrop for new issue activity also contributed to growth 

in commissions and fees revenue, which increased 25% 

year over year to $190 million. 

Our advisory business delivered a solid recovery as 

conditions improved, with continued progress on active 

mandates and pipeline development.  Advisory revenue 

increased modestly year over year to $312 million, 

supported by improved cross-border activity and our 

teams’ ability to capture opportunities across regions, 

reinforcing the strength and integration of our global 

platform. Technology remained the most active sector, 

accounting for 52% of advisory revenue. Although 

completion activity was impacted by a tariff-driven 

slowdown early in the year, and the recovery in small-

and mid-cap activity within our core sectors lagged 

the broader market, our teams continued to advance 

mandates and strengthen the pipeline, positioning 

the business to benefit as conditions became more 

constructive. During the year, we completed our 

acquisition of CRC-IB, a leading renewable energy 

advisory firm, enabling the formation of our new 

Energy Transformation group and deepening our 

platform capabilities in sustainability, energy transition 

and renewable infrastructure. 

During our third quarter, we completed the sale of our US 

wholesale market making business, allowing us to sharpen 

our focus on our integrated M&A and investment banking 

capital markets capabilities. While the divestiture has 

reduced trading revenues, it supports a transition toward 

a more balanced revenue mix, improving earnings visibility 

and contributing to a more favourable margin profile. 

We enter fiscal 2027 with a more focused and aligned 

capital markets business, supported by deep mid-market 

relationships and strong cross-border capabilities.  Client 

engagement remains strong and our pipeline continues 

to build, supporting our confidence in sustained capital 

raising and advisory activity. Following an exceptional 

period of mining-related activity, we expect a more 

balanced contribution across sectors, while recognizing 

that geopolitical uncertainty and market volatility may 

continue to affect transaction timing and volumes across 

our core regions.

Fourth quarter fiscal year 2026 for the three months and fiscal year ended March 31, 2026 – this document is dated June 3, 2026 The following discussion of the financial condition and results of operations for Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. is provided to enable the reader to assess material changes in our financial condition and to assess results for the three months and fiscal year ended March 31, 2026, compared to the corresponding periods in the preceding fiscal year. The three-month period ended March 31, 2026 is also referred to as fourth quarter fiscal 2026 and Q4/26. Unless otherwise indicated or the context otherwise requires, the “Company” refers to Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. and “Canaccord Genuity Group” refers to the Company and its direct and indirect subsidiaries. This discussion should be read in conjunction with the consolidated financial statements for the years ended March 31, 2026 and March 31, 2025 (“Annual Consolidated Financial Statements”). The Company’s Annual Consolidated Financial Statements are prepared in accordance with International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS). 

# Non-IFRS measures 

Certain non-IFRS measures, non-IFRS ratios and supplementary financial measures are utilized by the Company as measures of financial performance. Non-IFRS measures, non-IFRS ratios and supplementary financial measures do not have any standardized meaning prescribed by IFRS and are therefore unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other companies. Management believes that these non-IFRS measures, non-IFRS ratios and supplementary financial measures allow for a better evaluation of the operating performance of the Company’s business and facilitate meaningful comparison of results in the current period to those in prior periods and future periods. Non-IFRS measures presented in this MD&A include certain figures from our Consolidated Statement of Operations that are adjusted to exclude significant items. Although figures that exclude significant items provide useful information by excluding certain items that may not be indicative of the Company’s core operating results, a limitation of utilizing these figures that exclude significant items is that the IFRS accounting effects of these items do, in fact, reflect the underlying financial results of the Company’s business. Accordingly, these effects should not be ignored in evaluating and analyzing the Company’s financial results. Therefore, management believes that the Company’s IFRS measures of financial performance and the respective non-IFRS measures should be considered together. 

# Non-IFRS Measures (Adjusted Figures) 

Figures that exclude significant items provide useful information by excluding certain items that may not be indicative of the Company’s core operating results. Financial statement items that exclude significant items are non-IFRS measures. To calculate these non-IFRS financial statement items, we exclude certain items from our financial results prepared in accordance with IFRS. The items that have been excluded are referred to herein as significant items. The following is a description of the composition of the non-IFRS measures used in this MD&A (note that some significant items excluded may not be applicable to the calculation of the non-IFRS measure for each comparative period): (i) revenue excluding significant items, which is revenue per IFRS excluding any applicable fair value adjustments on certain illiquid or restricted marketable securities, warrants and options as recorded for IFRS reporting purposes but which are excluded for management reporting purposes and are not used by management to assess operating performance; (ii) expenses excluding significant items, which is expenses per IFRS less any applicable amortization of intangible assets acquired in connection with a business combination, acquisition-related expense items, which includes costs recognized in relation to both prospective and completed acquisitions, restructuring expenses, certain incentive-based costs related to the acquisitions and growth initiatives of Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management in the UK & Crown Dependencies (“CGWM UK”) and the US and UK capital markets divisions, fair value adjustment of certain contingent consideration in connection with prior acquisitions, fair value adjustments to the derivative liability component of non-controlling interests in CGWM UK; fair value adjustments to the derivative liability component related to the convertible debentures; certain expenses related to leased premises under construction; a fair value adjustment in respect of the CGWM UK management incentive plan; certain provisions and professional fees related to the US regulatory matters; certain provisions in connection with a tax matter related to previous fiscal years; impairment of goodwill in the US capital markets business; and net gain on disposal of the US wholesale market making business; (iii) overhead expenses excluding significant items, which are calculated as expenses excluding significant items less compensation expense; (iv) net income before taxes after intersegment allocations and excluding significant items, which is composed of revenue excluding significant items less expenses excluding significant items; (v) income taxes (adjusted), which is composed of income taxes per IFRS adjusted to reflect the associated tax effect of the excluded significant items, and also excludes the impairment of deferred tax assets in the US capital markets business; (vi) net income excluding significant items, which is net income before income taxes excluding significant items less income taxes (adjusted); (vii) non-controlling interests (adjusted), which is composed of the non-controlling interests per IFRS less the amortization of the equity component of the non-controlling interests in CGWM UK and adjusted as applicable under the treasury stock method when dilutive; (viii) net income attributable to common shareholders excluding significant items, which is net income excluding significant items less non-controlling interests (adjusted) and preferred share dividends paid on the Series A and Series C Preferred Shares. Other non-IFRS measures include earnings before income taxes, interest, depreciation and amortization (EBIDTA), which is net income before taxes excluding significant items and also excludes certain corporate interest revenue and corporate interest expense, depreciation and amortization and normalized EBITDA which is EBITDA excluding certain expenses of a specialized or non-recurring nature. EBITDA does not exclude right of use assets amortization and lease interest expense. The respective figures as described in this paragraph for the Company’s operating divisions are determined as described herein and are non-IFRS measures. 14 Management’s Discussion and Analysis

# Non-IFRS Ratios 

Non-IFRS ratios are calculated using the non-IFRS measures defined above. For the periods presented herein, we have used the following non-IFRS ratios: (i) total expenses excluding significant items as a percentage of revenue, which is calculated by dividing expenses excluding significant items by revenue excluding significant items; (ii) earnings per common share excluding significant items, which is calculated by dividing net income attributable to common shareholders excluding significant items by the weighted average number of common shares outstanding (basic); (iii) diluted earnings per common share excluding significant items, which is calculated by dividing net income attributable to common shareholders excluding significant items by the weighted average number of common shares outstanding (diluted), and (iv) pre-tax profit margin, which is calculated by dividing net income before taxes excluding significant items by revenue excluding significant items. 

# Supplementary Financial Measures 

Client assets are supplementary financial measures that do not have any definitions prescribed under IFRS and do not meet the definition of a non-IFRS measure or non-IFRS ratio. Client assets, which include both Assets under Management (AUM) and Assets under Administration (AUA), is a measure that is common to the wealth management business. Client assets are the market value of client assets managed and administered by the Company from which the Company earns interest, commissions and fees. This measure includes funds held in client accounts as well as the aggregate market value of long and short security positions. The Company’s method of calculating client assets may differ from the methods used by other companies and therefore these measures may not be comparable to other companies. Management uses these measures to assess operational performance of the Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management business segment. 

# Business Overview 

Through its principal subsidiaries, Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. is a leading independent, full-service financial services firm, with operations in two principal segments of the securities industry: wealth management and capital markets. Since its establishment in 1950, the Company has been driven by an unwavering commitment to building lasting client relationships. We achieve this by generating value for our private, institutional and corporate clients through comprehensive investment solutions, brokerage services, advisory and investment banking services. Canaccord Genuity Group has wealth management offices located in Canada, the UK, Guernsey, Jersey, the Isle of Man and Australia. Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets, the Company’s international capital markets division, operates in North America, the UK & Europe, Asia and Australia. Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. is publicly traded under the symbol CF on the Toronto Stock Exchange (TSX). Canaccord Genuity Series A Preferred Shares are listed on the TSX under the symbol CF.PR.A. Canaccord Genuity Series C Preferred Shares are listed on the TSX under the symbol CF.PR.C. 

ABOUT CANACCORD GENUITY GROUP INC.’S OPERATIONS 

Canaccord Genuity Group Inc.’s operations are divided into two business segments: Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets and Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management. Together, these operations offer a wide range of complementary advisory and investment banking services, investment products and brokerage services to the Company’s institutional, corporate and private clients. The Company’s administrative segment is referred to as Corporate and Other. 

Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets 

Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets is the global capital markets division of Canaccord Genuity Group Inc. (TSX: CF), offering institutional, corporate and government clients idea-driven investment banking, advisory mergers and acquisitions (M&A), research, sales and trading services with capabilities in North America, the UK & Europe, Asia, and Australia. We are committed to providing value-driven services to our clients throughout the entire lifecycle of their business and operating with high standards of excellence as an independent investment bank – expansive in resources and reach, but targeted in industry expertise, market focus and individual client attention. 

Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management 

Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management operations provide comprehensive wealth management solutions, brokerage and financial planning services to individual investors, private clients, charities and intermediaries through a full suite of services tailored to the needs of clients in each of its markets. The Company’s wealth management division has Investment Advisors (IAs) and professionals in Canada, the UK, Jersey, Guernsey, the Isle of Man and Australia. Guernsey, Jersey and the Isle of Man are together referred to as the Crown Dependencies. Our wealth management operations in the UK and in the Crown Dependencies are together referred to as CGWM UK. Management’s Discussion and Analysis 15 2026 ANNUAL REPORT / CANACCORD GENUITY GROUP INC. Corporate and Other 

Canaccord Genuity Group’s administrative segment, described as Corporate and Other, includes revenue and expenses associated with providing correspondent brokerage services, bank and other interest, and activities not specifically allocable to either the Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets or Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management divisions. Also included in this segment are the Company’s operations and support services, which are responsible for front- and back-office information technology systems, compliance, risk management, operations, legal, finance, and other administrative functions of Canaccord Genuity Group. 

BUSINESS ACTIVITY 

Our business is affected by the overall condition of the worldwide debt and equity markets. The Company’s revenue-generating activities are diversified across industry sectors and geographies. The Company has continued to invest in increasing the scale of its wealth management operations to enhance its recurring revenue base while growing its capital markets advisory capabilities to mitigate the inherent volatility associated with corporate financing activities in its core focus sectors. Historically, the Company’s diversification across major financial centres has allowed it to benefit from strong equity market performance in certain regions and improve its capability for identifying and servicing opportunities across core focus sectors in all geographies. The timing of revenue recognition can also materially affect the Company’s quarterly results. The majority of revenue from corporate financing and advisory transactions is recorded when the transaction has closed and, as a result, quarterly and annual results can also be affected by the timing for the recognition of revenue for such transactions in our capital markets business. IMPACT OF CHANGES IN MARKET ACTIVITY 

As a brokerage firm, the Company derives its revenue primarily from sales commissions and account fees, interest, underwriting, advisory fees, and trading activity. As a result, the Company’s business is materially affected by conditions in the financial marketplace and the economic environment, primarily in North America and the UK & Europe, Australia and to some degree, Asia. Canaccord Genuity Group’s disciplined corporate development initiatives over the past several years have laid a solid foundation for revenue diversification. A disciplined capital strategy allows the Company to remain competitive in a dynamic financial landscape. The Company’s wealth management activities are primarily focused on providing services related to discretionary and advisory account management, execution, financial planning, custody, margin loans and cash management. The value of client assets in the Company’s wealth management business can be impacted by changes in market values during reporting periods. The Company’s capital markets activities are primarily focused in the following sectors: Technology, Metals & Mining, Consumer & Retail, Healthcare & Life Sciences (which includes cannabis-related companies), Sustainability, and Other. Coverage of these sectors includes investment banking, mergers and acquisitions (M&A) and advisory services, and institutional equity activities, such as sales, trading, and research. 16 Management’s Discussion and Analysis

Canaccord Genuity Wealth Group Holdings (Jersey) Limited. Terms of the Convertible Preferred Shares and Preference Shares are disclosed in Note 8 of the Annual Consolidated Financial Statements. During the fourth fiscal quarter of fiscal 2026, the Company’s holding company for its Australian operations completed a rights offering of its ordinary shares. In connection with the rights offering, the Company’s beneficial ownership interest in the Australian operations decreased from 65.0% to 52.4%. For accounting purposes, the Company holds a 54.6% ownership reflecting shares held in an employee trust controlled by the Company’s Australian holding company [March 31, 2025 – 68.4%]. 

# Market Environment Economic backdrop 

Global equity markets were mixed during our fourth fiscal quarter. The S&P 500 declined 4.3% sequentially, while the S&P/TSX Composite Index, supported by greater exposure to commodities, generated a total return of 3.9%. On a year-over-year basis, global equity market performance remained constructive. US equities increased 17.8% year-over-year, while international equities, including the S&P/TSX (+34.8%) and emerging markets (+31.4%), contributed positively to the performance of the MSCI World Equity Index, which increased 20.5%. Commodity prices increased sharply during the quarter, particularly crude oil, reflecting heightened geopolitical tensions in the Middle East and disruption risk related to the Strait of Hormuz. At the same time, low inventory levels relative to business sales supported a re-stocking cycle, contributing to renewed inflationary pressure. Together, these factors prompted a more hawkish tone from both the Federal Reserve and the Bank of Canada. While certain leading areas of the economy appeared to be re-accelerating, labour and housing markets showed signs of softening. Despite higher commodity prices, the Canadian dollar depreciated 1.4% against the U.S. dollar, while U.S. 10-year Treasury bonds ended the quarter essentially flat, declining 0.1%. 

# Investment banking and advisory 

Industry data indicate that global M&A activity moderated during the three-month period, declining 10.6% sequentially, while remaining 29% above year-ago levels. Global IPO activity showed a similar trend, with a 29% quarter-over-quarter decline but a 52% increase compared to the prior-year period. Looking ahead, conditions across several of our core sectors remain constructive. Elevated commodity prices continue to support activity in energy and metals and mining, while improving small- and mid-cap equity performance and a more constructive, though still selective, investor risk appetite should support financing and advisory activity more broadly. Continued demand for technology-enabled growth, energy transition investment and secure supply chains may also create opportunities for strategic and cross-border transactions, although geopolitical uncertainty and technological disruption could contribute to periods of volatility. The Company’s capital-raising and advisory activities are primarily focused on small- and mid-capitalization companies in specific growth sectors of the global economy. These sectors may experience growth or downturns independent of broader economic and market conditions. Government regulation can also have a more profound impact on capital formation for smaller companies. Volatility in the business environment for these industries, or in the market for securities of companies within these industries in the regions where we operate could adversely affect our financial results and ultimately, the market value of our common shares. Advisory revenue is primarily recognized upon the successful completion of merger, acquisition and restructuring mandates and can vary significantly based on the timing of transaction closings. 

# Trading 

Trading activity in the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026 was supported by strong commodity performance and improved conditions in small- and mid-cap equity markets. # Global wealth management 

Despite periods of market volatility during the quarter, including geopolitical uncertainty and some valuation pressure in the UK, client assets in our wealth management businesses increased further in the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026. This growth was achieved over a mixed market backdrop with major equity indices across Canada, the US, the UK and Australia moving unevenly when compared to the prior three-month period. Increased volatility also supported client activity levels, which contributed to an increase in commissions and fees revenue in our wealth management business. # Fiscal 2027 Outlook 

The global economic environment continues to be influenced by trade policy uncertainty, geopolitical developments and evolving expectations for interest rates. The prospect of a more durable resolution to conflict in the Middle East is constructive for global confidence and financial markets. That said, recent increases in commodity prices and bond yields, together with U.S. dollar strength have contributed to tighter financial conditions which may weigh on global growth momentum. With that in mind, we anticipate the impact of elevated commodity prices on consumption to become more evident over the coming quarters. In the near term, resilient manufacturing indicators, inventory restocking and continued investment in artificial intelligence infrastructure are expected to provide support to economic activity. These factors may help offset some pressure from softer consumer demand, although stronger-than-expected core inflation or sustained increases in bond yields could limit the ability of central banks to lower interest rates. Against this backdrop, we expect conditions to remain generally supportive for commodity prices and small- and mid-capitalization equity markets. These conditions should contribute to improved capital raising, advisory and trading activity across our core capital markets focus sectors, while also supporting client assets and engagement within our wealth management businesses. A positive resolution to US-Canada trade negotiations would remove an important source of uncertainty and could improve prospects for cross-border activity across our platform.

# CANACCORD GENUITY WEALTH MANAGEMENT 

The Company’s combined global wealth management operations earned revenue of $306.7 million during the fourth fiscal quarter, representing a year-over-year increase of 28.4%. The increase was largely attributable to higher commissions and fees revenue, driven by higher average client assets from market appreciation and net new asset inflows. In addition, investment banking revenue in the Canadian and Australian operations increased stemming from increased retail participation in new issues. Enhanced year-over-year performance in the Australian operation was also attributable to the acquisition of Wilsons Advisory (“Wilsons”), which was completed on October 1, 2025. For fiscal 2026, consolidated wealth management revenue amounted to $1.1 billion, an increase of 24.2% compared to the prior year. Excluding significant items, this division recorded net income before taxes of $45.2 million (1) for the fourth quarter and $195.2 million (1) for the full fiscal year, representing year-over-year increases of 9.6% and 31.0% respectively. • Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management (North America) generated fiscal fourth quarter revenue of $130.3 million, primarily attributable to higher commissions and fees revenue reflecting higher client assets from market appreciation and new asset inflows, as well as higher investment banking revenue. Commissions and fees revenue and investment banking revenue increased by 26.9% and 143.1%, respectively, compared to the same period of the prior year. After intersegment allocations and excluding significant items, this business recorded net income before taxes of $18.8 million (1) for the three-month period, an increase of $6.1 million or 47.9% compared to Q4/25. Fiscal 2026 revenue in this business increased by 22.8% to $460.4 million and excluding significant items, net income before taxes and after intersegment allocations amounted to $69.5 million (1) , an increase of 61.4% compared to the prior fiscal year. • Wealth management operations in the UK & Crown Dependencies generated fourth quarter revenue of $127.4 million, a year-over-year increase of 8.3%, primarily attributable to an increase in commissions and fees revenue of $11.4 million reflecting market appreciation and net new asset inflows, partially offset by lower interest revenue. After intersegment allocations and excluding significant items, this business recorded net income before taxes of $23.1 million (1) in the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026, a decline of 16.3% compared to the same period of the prior year. The decline was primarily driven by a higher compensation ratio due to increased fixed compensation to support higher headcount as well higher non-compensation expenses to support the expanded operations and growth initiatives. Fiscal 2026 revenue in this business amounted to $512.8 million and excluding significant items, net income before taxes and after intersegment allocations amounted to $110.4 million (1) , increases of 14.0% and 9.3% respectively. • Wealth management operations in Australia generated fourth quarter revenue of $49.0 million, reflecting a year-over-year increase of 134.7%. Of this amount, $18.0 million was attributable to the acquisition of Wilsons. Compared to the same period of the prior year, commissions and fees revenue increased 132.9% to $44.5 million and investment banking revenue increased 182.4% to $4.0 million. After intersegment allocations and excluding significant items, this business recorded consolidated net income before taxes of $3.3 million (1) in the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026. Including contributions from the Wilsons acquisition since October 1, 2025, fiscal 2026 revenue in this business amounted to $150.1 million and net income before taxes and after intersegment allocations amounted to $15.3 million (1) , increases of 87.1% and 209.5%, respectively. Firm-wide client assets were $147.8 billion at March 31, 2026 representing an increase of $27.4 billion or 22.8% from $120.4 billion (2) at March 31, 2025. Client assets across the individual businesses as at March 31, 2026 were as follows: • $55.7 billion in North America, an increase of $13.0 billion or 30.4% from March 31, 2025 (2) .• $74.1 billion (£40.3 billion) in the UK & Crown Dependencies, an increase of $4.9 billion or 7.0% from $69.2 billion (£37.2 billion) at March 31, 2025. (2) • $18.0 billion in Australia held through our investment management platform, an increase of $9.6 billion or 113.2% from March 31, 2025 (2) . Approximately $7.0 billion of this increase was due to the acquisition of Wilsons, which was completed on October 1, 2025. (1) Figures excluding significant items are non-IFRS measures. # CANACCORD GENUITY CAPITAL MARKETS 

On a consolidated basis Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets earned revenue of $291.6 million for the fourth fiscal quarter, a year-over-year increase of 37.3%, primarily due to stronger contributions from investment banking and advisory activities, which increased by 161.3% and 32.0%, respectively. Excluding significant items, this segment recorded net income before income taxes of $58.3 million (1) for the three-month period, compared to $1.0 million in the same period of the prior fiscal year. For fiscal 2026, consolidated capital markets revenue excluding significant items (1) increased 25.8% year-over-year to $1.0 billion and pre-tax net income excluding significant items (1) and intersegment allocations increased by 221.9% to $140.9 million for the full fiscal year. Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets participated in a total of 114 investment banking transactions globally, raising total proceeds of $12.8 billion during the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026. Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets participated in a total of 472 investment banking transactions globally, raising total proceeds of $63.2 billion during fiscal 2026. # SUMMARY OF CORPORATE DEVELOPMENTS 

• During the first quarter of fiscal 2026, subsidiaries of the Company (“CG Group”) made approximately $27.0 million in new purchase loans to executive officers, senior managers and senior revenue producing employees of CG Group (the “Participants”) for the purpose of funding part of their purchase price for limited partnership units (“LP Units”) in CG Partners Limited Partnership (the “Partnership” or “CG Partners”), the independent employee share ownership partnership. In connection therewith, in June 2025, the Company advanced the Partnership a short-term interest-bearing secured loan in the principal amount of $17.0 million (“Partnership Loan”), which the Partnership repaid prior to June 30, 2025 using the cash proceeds that it received from the Participants’ subscription for LP Units. Prior to the end of the first quarter of fiscal 2027, CG Group is expected to advance new purchase loans (“New Purchase Loans”) to Participants to fund part of the purchase price of subscribing for additional LP Units and, in connection therewith, the Company is expected to advance a new short-term interest-bearing secured loan to the Partnership (the “New Partnership Loan”). See “Financial Condition” on page 46. • On September 22, 2025, John Albright was appointed to the Company’s board of directors as an Independent Director. With this appointment, Canaccord Genuity Group Inc.’s Board now comprises six directors, of whom five are independent. • On October 1, 2025, the Company, through its Australian operations, completed its acquisition of all of the equity interests in the holding company of Wilsons. • On November 7, 2025, the Company, through its US capital markets business, completed its previously announced agreement to sell its US wholesale market making business, further strengthening its focus on its core global advisory and equity capital markets (ECM)-led investment banking platform. • On January 14, 2026, the Company announced that through its US capital market business it has acquired Carbon Reduction Capital, LLC (“CRC”), a leading provider of M&A project finance and capital raising services in the US across the renewable energy spectrum with dedicated experience in the wind, solar, storage, carbon capture and energy transition segments. • On March 6, 2026, the Company entered into final settlement agreements with each of the US Securities and Exchange Commissions (“SEC”), the Financial Industry Regulatory Authority (“FINRA”), and the Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (“FinCEN”), resolving all previously disclosed enforcement actions related to these matters. The final settlement is $109.4 million (US$80.0 million), of which $6.8 million (US$5.0 million) is subject to suspension pending the delivery of a satisfactory suspicious activity reporting lookback review pursuant to the terms of the FinCEN Consent Order. As of March 31, 2026, the Company has paid $102.6 million (US$75.0 million) of the settlement amount, consistent with expectations, with the remaining suspended amount not payable if the lookback review is deemed satisfactory. Foreign exchange 

Revenue and expenses from the Company’s foreign operations are initially recorded in their respective functional currencies and translated into Canadian dollars using the prevailing exchange rates during the period. As a result, fluctuations in foreign exchange rates have impacted certain revenue and expense items when reported in Canadian dollars compared to the applicable prior periods. These effects should be considered when reviewing the following discussion of consolidated results as well as the discussion in respect of Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets and Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management divisions. 

Goodwill 

The Company conducted a goodwill impairment test as of March 31, 2026, using management’s estimates for revenue, operating performance, growth rates, and other assumptions typically required in discounted cash flow models in accordance with IAS 36 Impairment of Assets. As disclosed in the Q2 fiscal 2026 MD&A, the Company recorded a non-cash goodwill impairment charge of $110.0 million related to the US cash-generating-unit. This charge was recognized in Q2/26 and continues to impact year-to-date results. There were no additional impairments in Q4/26, and assumptions used in the impairment test remain unchanged. Refer to Note 13 of the Annual Consolidated Financial Statements for additional disclosures. The Company determined that there was no impairment in the goodwill associated with any of its wealth management business units in the UK & Crown Dependencies and Australia or its goodwill in Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets UK as of March 31, 2026. The Company will continue to reassess the carrying amount of goodwill at each reporting date.

Consolidated revenue in the Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets division increased by $79.3 million or 37.3% to $291.6 million in Q4/26 compared to the same quarter in the prior year, primarily driven by higher investment banking, advisory fees and commissions and fees revenue, partially offset by the decline in principal trading revenue in connection with the sale of the US wholesale market making business, which was completed on November 7, 2025. For fiscal 2026, consolidated revenue excluding significant items (1) increased by $214.6 million or 25.8% to $1.0 billion compared to the fiscal year ended March 31, 2025, also driven by higher investment banking and commissions and fees revenue. Revenue in the Company’s global wealth management operations amounted to $306.7 million and $1.1 billion for the three and 12-month periods ended March 31, 2026, increases of 28.4% and 24.2% respectively when compared to the same periods a year ago. The increases were primarily attributable to higher commissions and fees revenue earned in all regions, as well as higher investment banking revenue earned in the Canadian and Australian operations. Commissions and fees revenue is primarily generated from private client investment management trading activity and institutional sales and trading. Firm-wide revenue generated from commissions and fees in Q4/26 increased by $69.1 million or 29.2% year-over-year to a quarterly record of $305.9 million, primarily reflecting a 30.3% increase in revenue contributed by the wealth management division. Fiscal 2026 commissions and fees revenue was $1.1 billion, an increase of 26.0% or $226.9 million compared to a year ago and reflecting growth across all regions and a 26.4% increase in revenue from the wealth management division. Firm-wide investment banking revenue increased by $76.6 million or 159.4% to $124.7 million in Q4/26 compared to Q4/25, primarily reflecting increased corporate financing activities in the Canada, Australia and the UK capital markets operations, partially offset by a decline in our US capital markets operations. Additionally, the fourth quarter investment banking contribution from the wealth management division increased by $11.9 million or 150.1% year-over-year to $19.9 million, due to increased activity in our Canadian and Australian businesses. Firm-wide investment banking revenue for the fiscal year ended March 31, 2026 amounted to $483.4 million, a year-over-year increase of $236.9 million or 96.1% as revenue improved across all the regions and businesses. Advisory fees revenue for the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026 increased by $29.1 million or 32.2% compared to the same quarter in the prior year. Our US capital markets operations were the largest contributor of fourth quarter advisory revenue of $58.0 million, representing year-over-year growth of 5.8% while revenue contributed by the Canadian capital markets operations increased 313.1% year-over-year to $42.3 million, primarily due to significant transactions completed during the quarter. In contrast, advisory fees revenue in the UK business declined by $7.0 million or 28.4% in the fourth quarter compared to the same period in the prior year. Firm-wide advisory fees revenue for the 12 months ended March 31, 2026 increased by 2.2% or $6.8 million to $313.8 million. Firm-wide principal trading revenue amounted to $6.7 million and $91.8 million for the three-and 12-month periods ended March 31, 2026, representing decreases of 79.3% and 23.4% compared to the same periods in the prior year. These decreases were largely due to lower revenue earned from our US operations, reflecting the sale of the US wholesale market making business which was completed on November 7, 2025. Firm-wide interest revenue was $48.3 million and $189.9 million for the three- and 12-month periods ended March 31, 2026. Interest revenue was relatively unchanged on a quarterly basis and decreased by $15.7 million or 7.6% on a fiscal year basis. The decline in the 12-month period ended March 31, 2026 was primarily attributable to lower client interest revenue in our UK and Canadian wealth management operations, reflecting the impact of lower interest rates, as well as reduced stock loan activity in the Canadian capital markets operations. Other revenue was $11.0 million for Q4/26 and $59.9 million for fiscal 2026, compared to $5.5 million and $17.7 million, respectively, in the corresponding prior year periods. Fiscal 2026 other revenue included a $19.9 million gain on disposal of the US wholesale market making business, which was completed on November 7, 2025 and excluded as a significant item on an adjusted basis (1) . Excluding this item, the increase in other revenue was partially attributable to higher revenue earned from the correspondent brokerage business and changes in the fair value adjustments on certain illiquid or restricted marketable securities. 

EXPENSES 

Firm-wide expenses for the three months ended March 31, 2026 amounted to $518.2 million, an increase of 17.0% or $75.2 million from Q4/25. The increase primarily reflects higher compensation expense associated with increased revenue and an increase in the fair value of the convertible debentures derivative liability, which is treated as a significant item on an adjusted basis (1) . These increases were partially offset by a 13.2% decrease in interest expense, reflecting the impact of lower interest rates and a decrease in the fair value of the non-controlling interests derivative liability, which is treated as a significant item on an adjusted basis (1) . Development costs decreased overall by $5.2 million, primarily due to a reduction in the fair value of the CGWM UK management incentive plan, which is treated as a significant item on an adjusted basis (1) , partially offset by higher development costs in our US capital markets operations and UK wealth management business related to growth initiatives. Excluding significant items (1) , total expenses were $523.6 million, representing an increase of 22.4% year-over-year, driven primarily by higher compensation expense and higher development costs in the US and CGWM UK as discussed above. For the year ended March 31, 2026, total expenses were $2.2 billion, compared to $1.7 billion for the prior year, representing an increase of 30.9% year-over-year. The increase was primarily driven by higher compensation expense associated with increased revenue and higher development costs related to growth initiatives. Other overhead expenses for the fourth fiscal quarter were $162.1 million, a decrease of 4.2% compared to Q4/25 primarily related to a year-over-year reduction in development costs and lower interest expense as described below. As a percentage of revenue, other overhead expenses were 26.3% in Q4/26 compared to 36.7% in Q4/25, representing a decrease of 10.4 percentage points. Interest expense decreased by $3.6 million or 13.2%, partially due to lower interest rates and the sale of the US wholesale market marking business that was completed during Q3 fiscal 2026. Development costs also decreased by $5.2 million or 30.7%, primarily due to a lower fair value adjustment related to the CGWM UK management incentive plan, reflecting changes in market conditions impacting the valuation of the incentive plan, partially offset by higher costs related to growth initiatives in the US capital markets operations and in CGWM UK. General and administrative expense also decreased by $2.1 million or 4.2% due to lower professional fees in the Canadian and US capital markets operations, partially offset by higher costs in CGWM UK to support business growth. These decreases were partially offset by an increase in amortization expense of $3.1 million or 26.2% primarily due to amortization of intangible assets acquired in connection with previous acquisitions in the UK & Crown Dependencies wealth management operations, as well as the completion of the Wilsons acquisition in Australia on October 1, 2025. Other overhead expenses for the year ended March 31, 2026 increased by $90.6 million or 13.8% to $747.6 million, compared to fiscal 2025. The increase was primarily due to higher general and administrative expenses related to provisions and legal fees recorded in connection with the resolved US regulatory matters, as well as higher amortization expenses, primarily for the reasons discussed above. As a percentage of revenue, however, other overhead expenses decreased by 3.7 percentage points over the comparative period. In addition, development costs increased by $15.9 million or 26.9% to $74.9 million for the 12-month period ended March 31, 2026, largely due to fair value adjustments related to the CGWM UK management incentive plan and costs incurred in support of business growth in the US capital markets and CGWM UK. 

Other expenses 

During the three and 12 months ended March 31, 2026, the Company recorded non-cash fair value adjustments of $8.1 million and $29.5 million, respectively, related to the derivative liability component of the convertible debentures. In addition, during the three and 12 months ended March 31, 2026, the Company recorded non-cash fair value adjustments related to the derivative liability component of the non-controlling interests associated with the Convertible Preferred Shares and Preference Shares issued by CGWM UK. These adjustments resulted in a $19.0 million decrease in fair value in Q4 fiscal 2026. For the full fiscal year, however, changes in fair value resulted in a $9.0 million expense. The decrease in the fourth quarter was primarily driven by changes in valuation assumptions arising from changes in market conditions. In Q4 fiscal 2026, the Company recorded a $2.0 million reduction in the fair value of contingent consideration associated with prior acquisitions. For the full fiscal year, however, increases in fair value resulted in a $3.9 million expense. 

INCOME TAX 

Income tax expense for the three months ended March 31, 2026 was $11.5 million on net income before income taxes of $97.7 million compared to income tax expense of $7.4 million on net income before income taxes of $18.3 million in Q4/25. For the fiscal year ended March 31, 2026, income tax expense was $70.3 million on a loss before income taxes of $8.5 million compared to income tax expense of $28.4 million on net income before income taxes of $53.5 million for the prior year. The Company recognizes deferred tax assets for all deductible temporary differences and unused tax losses, to the extent that it is probable that taxable profit will be available against which these can be utilized. As disclosed in the Company’s second quarter fiscal 2026 MD&A, the Company recorded a non-cash impairment of deferred tax assets amounting to $16.0 million related to its US capital markets business, which was recognized through income tax expense in the second quarter of fiscal 2026. During the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026, as a result of improved future cash flow projections following the acquisition of CRC, the Company reversed $12.1 million of the previously recorded deferred tax asset valuation allowance, which was also recognized through income tax expense. In addition to the impairment of US deferred tax assets, the effective tax rate for the three and 12 months ended March 31, 2026 was also impacted by certain expenses that were not deductible for tax purposes including fair value adjustments of the derivative liabilities, fair value adjustment related to the CGWM UK management incentive plan and the provision related to the resolved US regulatory matters recorded in the US capital markets business. Pillar Two legislation, which introduces a global minimum corporate tax rate of 15% for multinational enterprises surpassing certain revenue thresholds, has been enacted or substantively enacted in certain jurisdictions in which the Company operates through its subsidiaries. For the year ended March 31, 2026, the Company has recognized Pillar Two current tax expense of $3.7 million primarily relating to qualified domestic minimum top-up taxes in certain jurisdictions with low statutory tax rates.

Canaccord Genuity Group’s operations are divided into three segments: Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets and Canaccord Genuity Wealth Management are the principal operating segments while Corporate and Other is mainly an administrative segment. 

# Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets 

Overview Management views the Company’s capital markets businesses in Canada, the US, UK & Europe and Australia as a single, integrated business, and as such, the discussions of Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets below and throughout the MD&A are on a consolidated basis. Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets provides a full range of investment banking, advisory, equity research, and sales and trading services to corporate, institutional and government clients and also conducts principal trading activities. The Company has offices and employees in 19 offices in Canada, the US, the UK & Europe, Australia, and Asia. Our capital markets division has 852 employees who are organized into product, industry, geographic and support groups. Our industry coverage groups are focused in key growth sectors of the global economy. Primary focus sectors are Technology, Metals & Mining, Consumer & Retail, Healthcare & Life Sciences (which includes cannabis-related companies), and Sustainability & Structured Products. Additional sectors covered include Diversified, Transportation & Industrials, and Energy. Our capabilities include private placements, equity and debt underwriting, initial public offerings, follow-on offerings, at-the-market offerings, debt finance and restructuring, advisory (which includes mergers, acquisitions, and private capital/financial sponsor advisory services), principal trading, block trades, research and market making. A disciplined mid-market focus with global alignment efforts have firmly entrenched Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets as a leading global independent investment bank specializing in its core focus sectors and geographies. The Company believes that Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets’ integrated global platform and disciplined focus in key growth sectors of the global economy provide a competitive advantage. Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets is focused on providing execution capabilities and specialized knowledge within its core focus sectors across geographies, providing a differentiated service when compared to other global investment banks. 

Outlook 

Canaccord Genuity Capital Markets continues to take steps to advance its market position as a mid-market leader in many of the Company’s key markets. Investment banking and advisory activities are focused on core sectors where the Company aims to establish leadership across its operating geographies. Integrated cross-border capabilities enable each of the Company’s regional capital markets operations to differentiate from domestically focused peers. Throughout fiscal 2026, the metals and mining sector was the largest contributor to corporate financing revenue. However, activity levels across other core sectors improved over the course of the fiscal year. Similarly, while advisory activity has been concentrated in the technology sector, it has begun to diversify. 30 Management’s Discussion and Analysis  

> CANACCORD GENUITY GROUP INC. / 2026 ANNUAL REPORT

The dynamic nature of the global operating environment for global mid-market companies and investors requires the Company to maintain a level of agility in its business mix that allows it to stay competitive and meet the evolving needs of clients. For this reason, the Company will continue to make disciplined investments, as appropriate, to further strengthen its operations in areas where additional market share can be captured. The Company remains committed to operating its capital markets businesses as efficiently as possible in order to protect its capacity to deliver market-leading expertise and execution services throughout all market cycles. Management maintains a strong focus on capturing operating efficiencies and strengthening its global platform through further integration of its global capabilities and by further enhancing cross-border coordination among all of the Company’s geographies. A culture of cost containment continues to be reinforced throughout the Company, and strategies to lower operating costs in this division over the long term continue to be explored. Increasing contributions from higher-margin advisory activities continues to be an important strategic priority. Our specialized expertise in key sectors of the economy and track record of success in related equity capital markets activities position us well to unlock opportunities for our clients as they grow. We lead a wide variety of sell-side and buy-side strategic advisory mandates both domestically and cross border., The Company expects continued benefits from its investments to increase contributions from higher-margin advisory activities, supported by platform expansion through acquisitions since 2019, and most recently the acquisition of Carbon Reduction Capital, LLC, a leading advisor to the renewable energy sector, which was completed in the third quarter of fiscal 2026. The divestiture of the US wholesale market making business in fiscal 2026 further strengthens the Company’s focus on its core global advisory and ECM-led investment banking platform.

In Australia and Canada, revenue is also earned through warrant and fee share inventory positions that are included in investment banking revenue. The value of these positions fluctuates with changes in market prices.

EXPENSES – CANACCORD GENUITY CAPITAL MARKETS 

Total expenses in the capital markets division for the three months ended March 31, 2026 amounted to $236.2 million, representing an increase of 12.7% or $26.7 million compared to the same period a year ago. The increase was primarily driven by higher compensation expense, largely in connection with increased revenue, and higher development costs, partially offset by reductions in trading, settlement and technology costs, general and administrative, and interest expenses. For the year ended March 31, 2026, total expenses increased by $285.4 million or 35.7% to $1.1 billion. The fiscal year increase was primarily driven by a non-cash goodwill impairment charge of $110.0 million in the US capital markets business, higher provision and professional fees recorded in the US capital markets operations in connection with the resolved US regulatory matters, as well as higher compensation expense. Excluding significant items (1) , total expenses in the fourth fiscal quarter amounted to $228.1 million, reflecting an increase of 10.7% compared to Q4/25. For the fiscal year ended March 31, 2026, expenses increased by 15.2% year-over-year to $883.7 million. As a percentage of revenue, total expenses excluding significant items (1) decreased by 18.8 percentage points and by 7.8 percentage points for the three- and 12-month periods ended March 31, 2026, respectively, compared to the same periods in the prior year, reflecting decreases in all regions. Total non-compensation expenses as a percentage of revenue excluding significant items (1) decreased by 12.0 percentage points in Q4/26 and by 7.8 percentage points on a yearly basis, reflecting decreases across all regions. 

Compensation expense 

Compensation expense in our capital markets division for the three and 12 months ended March 31, 2026 increased by $32.3 million or 23.1% and $130.5 million or 25.8%, respectively, compared to the same periods in the prior year, largely in connection with increased revenue. Total compensation expense as a percentage of revenue for the three months ended March 31, 2026 was 59.1%, a decrease of 6.8 percentage points compared to Q4/25. For fiscal 2026, total compensation ratio was 59.7%, a decrease of 1.2 percentage points from the prior year. Excluding significant items (1) , total compensation expense as a percentage of revenue was 60.8% for the year ended March 31, 2026, largely unchanged from fiscal 2025. During the third quarter of fiscal 2026, the Company recorded a $19.9 million gain on the disposal of the US wholesale market making business, which was excluded as a significant item on an adjusted basis (1) . Accordingly, revenue was reduced and the compensation ratio increased on an adjusted basis (1) .In our Canadian capital markets operations, total compensation as a percentage of revenue decreased by 16.1 percentage points in Q4/26 compared with Q4/25 due to timing of bonus accruals. The compensation ratio in the Australian capital markets operations declined by 9.0 percentage points due to the significant increase in revenue relative to fixed staff costs. In the US, total compensation as a percentage of adjusted revenue increased by 2.2 percentage points, primarily due to the change in revenue mix following the sale of the US wholesale market making business on November 7, 2025. In the UK compensation ratio remained relatively unchanged from Q4 fiscal 2025, reflecting a slight increase of 0.5 percentage points.
